3.6.
Compact subgroups and base cycles

3.6.1.
Further notations

For any finite set
S
of finite places of our fixed totally real field
F
, set
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}
for the ring of
S
-units that is the set of
x\in F
such that
v(x)\geq 0
for all finite places
v\not\in S
and put
\mathcal{O}_{E}^{S}=\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}
. We will also use

F_{S}:=\prod_{v\in S}F_{v},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{S}:=\prod_{v\not\in S}^{\prime}F_{v}

the restricted product of the additive groups
F_{v}^{\times}
for all finite places
v\not\in S
, with respect to the local integers
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}^{\times}
. One can write the finite adeles of
F
as the product
\mathds{A}_{F,f}=F_{S}\times\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{S}
. For any place
v
of
F
, let
F_{v}
be the completion of
F
at
v
and let
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
be its ring of integers with uniformizer
\varpi_{v}
and maximal ideal
\mathfrak{p}_{v}=\varpi_{v}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
. For any
F
-algebra
R
, let
R_{v}=R\otimes_{F}F_{v}
.

Recall that we have fixed at the beginning of §
2
, for each finite place
v
of
F
, an embedding
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{v}\colon\overline{F}}
F
_v
and we set
w_{v}
for the unique place of
E
defined by
\iota_{v}
, If
v
splits in
E
, by abuse of notation denote the other place by
\overline{w}_{v}
. When the place
v
is understood from the context, we will omit the subscript
v
and simply write
w
and
\overline{w}
.

3.6.2.
Integral models

Recall that we identify
\mathbf{U}(W)
with the subgroup of
\mathbf{U}(V)
given by

\{g\in\mathbf{U}(V)(R)\subset\GL(V\otimes_{F}R)\colon g\cdot x=x,\quad\forall x\in D\otimes_{F}R\},

for any
F
-algebras
R
. We have a faithful representations of
\mathbf{U}(V)
and
\mathbf{U}(W)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{U}(W)}
U
(V)
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\GL(V_{F})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

where

is the underlying
F
-vector space of the hermitian
E
-space
(V,\psi)
(§

). We identify
\mathbf{U}_{V}
and
\mathbf{U}_{W}
with closed subgroups of

. Let
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{V}
and

be the schematic closures of
\mathbf{U}(V)
and
\mathbf{U}(W)
in
\mathcal{G}=\GL(V_{F})_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}

26

26

Here, we are picking up implicitly a lattice in
V_{F}
to obtain the
\mathcal{O}_{F}
structure.
.

For
\star\in\{V,W\}
, the schematic closure
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star}
is a model for
{\mathbf{U}}(\star)
over
\mathcal{O}_{F}

[
GH19
, Lemma 2.4.1]
. But by
[
GH19
, Lemma 2.4.2]
, there is a finite set

of finite places of

such that
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star,\mathcal{O}_{F}^{{S_{\star}^{0}}}}
becomes smooth over

. Subsequently,
[

, Proposition 3.1.9]
ensures that for a large enough finite set

of finite places of

containing

, all the fibers of

will be connected and reductive, i.e.
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star,\mathcal{O}_{F}^{{S_{\star}^{1}}}}
is a reductive
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{{S_{\star}^{1}}}
-model of
\mathbf{U}(\star)
. Accordingly, the homomorphism
\iota
extends to the models over

for

and we get

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W,\mathcal{O}_{F}^{{S}^{1}}}}

_V,

_F^S^1
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{F,S^{1}}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

Using
[

, Lemma 2.4.1]
, we get for any finite place

of

a hyperspecial maximal compact subgroup

\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{V}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})={\mathbf{U}}(V)(F_{v})\cap\mathcal{G}({\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}})\text{ and,}

(3.5)

\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})={\mathbf{U}}(W)(F_{v})\cap\mathcal{G}({\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}})={\mathbf{U}}(W)(F_{v})\cap\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{V}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}).

Remark 3.6.1
.

In the previous discussion we chose on purpose to use general arguments. Nevertheless, a more specific argument goes as follows: We pick an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
lattice
\mathcal{L}_{W}
in
W
,
\mathcal{L}_{D}
in
D
and set
\mathcal{L}_{V}=\mathcal{L}_{W}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{D}
in
V
. We choose them such that they are contained in their duals, with the quotients as small as possible. Now, we take
S^{1}
to be the bad places, those occurring in the quotient
\mathcal{L}_{V}^{\vee}/\mathcal{L}_{V}
. Away from
S^{1}
, the restriction of
\psi
on
\mathcal{L}_{V}
,
\mathcal{L}_{W}
is a perfect integral hermitian pairing, giving rise to the desired smooth reductive models which are unitary groups.

3.6.3.
Compact subgroups

Let
K_{\star}
, with
\star\in\{V,W\}
, be any open compact subgroup of
\mathbf{U}_{\star}(\mathds{A}_{F,f})
. It inersects then
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star}(\prod_{v\not\in S^{1}}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})=\prod_{v\not\in S^{1}}\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})
in an open compact subgroup. Therefore, there exists a finite set
S^{2}=S^{2}(K_{V},K_{W})
27
27

27

We want to insist here that the enlarged set
S
depends on both compacts
K_{V}
and
K_{W}
.
containing
S^{1}
, for which both compact subgroups can be written as

K_{\star}=K_{\star,S^{2}}\times\prod_{v\not\in S^{2}}\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}),\forall\star\in\{V,W\}

where
K_{\star,S^{2}}
is some open compact subgroup of
\mathbf{U}(\star)(F_{S^{2}})=\prod_{v\in S^{2}}\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star}({F_{v}})
. In particular, if we set
K_{\star,v}:=\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\star}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})
for all finite places
v
of
F
away from
S^{2}
, we have by (
3.5
)

K_{W,v}=\mathbf{U}(W)(F_{v})\cap K_{V,v}.

3.6.4.
A base cycle

Now for each
\star\in\{V,W\}
, let
g_{\star}=(g_{\star,v})
be any fixed element of
\mathbf{U}_{\star}(\mathds{A}_{f})
. By definition of the adelic points of
\mathbf{U}_{\star}
, there exists a finite set of places
S=S(K_{V},K_{W},g_{V},g_{W})
containing
S^{2}\cup\text{Ram}(E/F)
and such that
g_{\star,v}\in K_{\star,v}
for all
v\not\in S
and
g_{\star,S}\in\mathbf{U}_{\star}(F_{S})
. But as far as the formation of the cycle
\mathfrak{z}_{g}
for
g=(g_{V},g_{W})
is concerned, nothing is lost by considering
g_{0}=(g_{0,V},g_{0,W})\in\mathbf{G}(\mathds{A}_{f})
, such that
g_{0,\star}^{S}=1\in\mathbf{U}_{\star}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{S})
and
g_{0,\star,S}=g_{\star,S}\in\mathbf{U}_{\star}(F_{S})
, since
\mathfrak{z}_{g}=\mathfrak{z}_{g_{0}}
.

Now that we have fixed our set of finite places
S
, let us consider

\underline{\mathbf{T}}^{1}:=\ker\Norm\colon\underbrace{\Res_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{S}/\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}}\mathds{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{S}}}_{:=\underline{\mathbf{T}}}\to\underbrace{\mathds{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}}}_{:=\underline{\Zbf}}.

Each of the above reductive groups over
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}
are models for the obvious corresponding groups in
\mathbf{T}_{F}^{1},\mathbf{T}_{F}
and
\Zbf_{F}
, so in particular

\mathbf{T}^{1}(\mathds{A}_{f})=\underline{\mathbf{T}}^{1}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}),\mathbf{T}(\mathds{A}_{f})=\underline{\mathbf{T}}(\mathds{A}_{F,f})\text{ and }\Zbf(\mathds{A}_{f})=\underline{\Zbf}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}).

We view again
\underline{\mathbf{T}}^{1}
as the center of
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W}
and also
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{V}
. Let
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W}^{\der}
denote the kernel of the determinant map
\det\colon\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W}\to\underline{\mathbf{T}}^{1}
. Write
\underline{\nu}\colon\underline{\mathbf{T}}\twoheadrightarrow\underline{\mathbf{T}}^{1}
for the homomorphism given on
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}
-points by
z\mapsto z/\overline{z}
.

Define
\underline{\mathbf{G}}:=\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{V}\times\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W}
and
\underline{\mathbf{H}}:=\Delta(\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W})\subset\underline{\mathbf{G}}
28
28

28

The diagonal homomorphism
\Delta
extends to the models over
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}
since
\iota
does.
. We then have

\mathbf{G}(\mathds{A}_{f})=\underline{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})=\underline{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}).

Set
K_{v}:=K_{V,v}\times K_{W,v}
for any place
v\not\in S
,
K_{S}=K_{V,S}\times K_{W,S}
,
K^{S}=K_{V}^{S}\times K_{W}^{S}
.
K=K_{V}\times K_{W}
and
K_{\mathbf{H}}=\Delta(K_{W})=K_{\mathbf{H},S}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}
.

.

3.7.
The field
\mathcal{K}

The stabilizer of
\mathfrak{z}_{g_{0}}
in
\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})
is

\text{Stab}_{\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})}\mathfrak{z}_{g_{0}}=\left(Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cdot\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1}\right)\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f}).

Lemma 3.7.1
.

We may rewrite the stabilizer as

\text{Stab}_{\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})}\mathfrak{z}_{g_{0}}=\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot\left({K_{\mathbf{H},g_{0},S}^{Z}}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}\right),

where
K_{\mathbf{H},g_{0},S}^{Z}:=\left((Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S})\cdot g_{0,S}K_{S}g_{0,S}^{-1}\right)\cap{\mathbf{H}}(F_{S})
29
29

29

Note that by SV5 of Remark
2.6.2
the subgroup
Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})
is discrete in
Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{A}_{f})
and consequently the intersection
Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S}
must be finite.
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\text{Stab}_{\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})}\mathfrak{z}_{g_{0}}

\displaystyle{=}\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot\left((Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap Z_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{Q})K^{S})\cdot g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})\right)

\displaystyle{=}\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot\left((Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S})\cdot g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})\right)

\displaystyle{=}\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot\left({\left((Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S})\cdot g_{0,S}K_{S}g_{0,S}^{-1}\right)\cap{\mathbf{H}}(F_{S})}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot\left({K_{\mathbf{H},g_{0},S}^{Z}}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}\right),

where, (0)

is a straightforward consequence of the fact that
g_{0}^{S}=1\in\underline{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{S})
and
K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}=K^{S}\cap\underline{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{S})
.

(1)

Let
z_{G}=z_{H}k\in Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap Z_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{Q})K^{S}=Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap Z_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{Q})\underline{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S})
. Write
z_{G}=(z_{V},z_{W})
and
z_{H}k=\Delta(z_{W}^{\prime})(k_{V},k_{W})=kz_{H}
, hence
30

30

.

z_{V}w_{n+1}=k_{V}z_{W}^{\prime}w_{n+1}=k_{V}w_{n+1},

so

. Accordingly
z_{W}^{\prime}\in\underline{\mathbf{T}}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S})=Z_{\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W}}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S})
and
z_{G}\in K^{S}
, i.e.
Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap Z_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{Q})K^{S}=Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S}
.

(2)

Let
(zg_{0,S}k_{S}g_{0,S}^{-1},k^{S})\in K_{\mathbf{H},g_{0},S}^{Z}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}\subset\underline{\mathbf{H}}(\mathds{A}_{F,f})
, for some

. Hence

\displaystyle(zg_{0,S}k_{S}g_{0,S}^{-1},k^{S})

\displaystyle=z(g_{0,S}k_{S}g_{0,S}^{-1},\underbrace{z^{-1}k^{S}}_{:=k^{\prime S}\in K^{S}})

\displaystyle=z(g_{0,S}k_{S}g_{0,S}^{-1},k^{\prime S})

\displaystyle=zg_{0}(k_{S},k^{\prime S})g_{0}^{-1}\in(Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S})g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f}),

and so
((Z_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathds{Q})\cap K^{S})\cdot g_{0}Kg_{0}^{-1})\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})=K_{\mathbf{H},g_{0},S}^{Z}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S}
.∎

Set

, the field of definition of the cycle
\mathfrak{z}_{g_{0}}
, it is the subfield of
E_{g_{0}}\subset E(\infty)

31

and

.
fixed by (see §

3.8.
Ramification in transfer fields extensions

Let
v
be a finite place of
F
that is unramified in the extension
E/F
, hence

E_{v}^{\times}/F_{v}^{\times}\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}^{\times}\simeq\begin{cases}\varpi_{v}^{\mathds{Z}}&\text{If $v$ split in $E$,}\\
\{1\}&\text{If $v$ is inert in $E$.}\end{cases}

Lemma 3.8.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
be any non-zero ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
.
Any prime of
E
not dividing
\mathfrak{c}\mathcal{O}_{E}
is unramified in
E[\mathfrak{c}]/E
, hence also in
E(\mathfrak{c})/E
.

Proof.

Let
v
be any place of
E
and
E_{v}
the completion of
E
at the place
v
. The extension
E[\mathfrak{c}]/E
being Galois, then the various completions
E[\mathfrak{c}]_{w}
with
w
a place of
E[\mathfrak{c}]
extending
v
are isomorphic, let
E[\mathfrak{c}]_{w}
be (any) one of these completions. Let
\mathfrak{q}
be the prime of
E[\mathfrak{c}]
corresponding to the place
w
and identify the local Galois group
\text{Gal}(E[\mathfrak{c}]_{w}/E_{v})
with the decomposition group of
D_{\mathfrak{q}}(E[\mathfrak{c}]/E)\subset\text{Gal}(E[\mathfrak{c}]/E)
(the stabilizer of
\mathfrak{\mathfrak{q}}
in
\text{Gal}(E[\mathfrak{c}]/E)
).
The decomposition group
D_{\mathfrak{q}}(E[\mathfrak{c}]/E)
is the image of the composition of the following two maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{v}^{\times}}
A
_E,f^×
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\times}}
Gal
(E[
c
]/E),
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

and the image of
\mathcal{O}_{E,v}^{\times}
in
\text{Gal}(E[\mathfrak{c}]/E)
is precisely the inertia group
I(E[\mathfrak{c}]/E)
. Now, because
v\nmid\mathfrak{c}\mathcal{O}_{E}
we see that the image of
\mathcal{O}_{E,v}^{\times}
is trivial, i.e.
v
is unramified in the extension
E[\mathfrak{c}]/E
.
∎

3.9.
Ramification in
\mathcal{K}
-transfer fields extensions

For any nonzero ideal
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
that is prime to
S
, we consider the field
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})\subset E(\infty)
, fixed by

\Art^{1}_{E}(U_{\mathfrak{f}})
, where
U_{\mathfrak{f}}:=U_{g_{0},S}\times U_{\mathfrak{f}}^{S}
with
U_{\mathfrak{f}}^{S}=\prod_{v\not\in S}U_{v}^{1}({\text{ord}}_{F_{v}}(\mathfrak{f}))
and

U_{v}^{1}(c):=\underline{\nu}\big((\underbrace{\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}+\varpi_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}}_{:=\mathcal{O}_{v,c}})^{\times}\big),\quad v\not\in S,\,c\in\mathds{N}.

The fields
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
will be called the
\mathcal{K}
-transfer field of conductors
\mathfrak{f}
. Set

\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}:=\mathcal{O}_{g_{0},S}\times(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{F}^{S}})=\mathcal{O}_{g_{0},S}\times\prod_{v\not\in S}\mathcal{O}_{v,{\text{ord}}_{F_{v}}(\mathfrak{f})}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{E}},

in particular, we have
\underline{\nu}(\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times})=U_{\mathfrak{f}}
. Note that
\mathcal{K}(1)=\mathcal{K}
. Moreover, for every two

For later use, we set

Remark 3.9.2
.

Observe that by (
3.3
), for any ideal
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
prime to
S
, there exists a smallest non-zero ideal
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{f}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
with respect to divisibility such that

\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\subset\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{f}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{E}

Equivalently,
E(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{f}})
is the smallest transfer field containing
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
:

\displaystyle E(\mathfrak{f})

\displaystyle\subset\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})\subset E(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{f}}).\qed

Lemma 3.9.3
.

For any ideal
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
prime to
S
, the field
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
is contained in the transfer field
E(\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mathfrak{f})
.

Proof.

We have
\mathfrak{O}_{1}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}}=\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}
, but
\mathfrak{O}_{1}\supset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}
(Remark
3.9.2
). It follows that

\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}\supset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}}\cap\widehat{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\supset\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mathfrak{f}},

hence
E(\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mathfrak{f})\supset\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
.∎

Using Lemmas
3.8.1
and
3.9.3
we get the following immediate consequence:

Corollary 3.9.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
be a non-zero ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
prime to
S
. If
\mathfrak{p}
is a prime ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
not dividing
\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mathfrak{f}
then each prime of
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
is unramified in
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
.

Lemma 3.9.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{f}
be any non-zero ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
satisfying

\mathfrak{f}\mathcal{O}_{E}\nmid I_{0}:=\text{lcm}\{(u-1)\colon u\in(\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times})_{\text{tors}},u\neq 1\}.

For any ideal
\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
, we have

{E^{\times}\cap\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{\times}\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}}\simeq F^{\times}\quad{\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\times}\cap\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}.

Proof.

This is
[
Nek07
, Proposition 2.10]
.∎

Applying Lemma
3.9.5
to (
3.6
) yields an isomorphism of groups:

Corollary 3.9.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{f}
be any non-zero ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
prime to
S
and
\mathfrak{f}\mathcal{O}_{E}\nmid I_{0}
. For any
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-ideal
\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}
, we have:

\text{Gal}(\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{f})/\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f}))\simeq\dfrac{\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}}{\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}}\simeq\prod_{v\in\text{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{F})\colon\mathfrak{p}_{v}\mid{\mathfrak{n}}}\frac{\mathcal{O}_{v,{\text{ord}}_{F_{v}}(\mathfrak{f})}^{\times}}{\mathcal{O}_{v,{\text{ord}}_{F_{v}}(\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{n})}^{\times}}.

3.10.
Interlude on orders

Define the Artin symbol,

\left(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{E}}{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}\right)=\begin{cases}-1&\text{ if $\mathfrak{p}_{v}$ remains inert in $E$,}\\
1&\text{ if $\mathfrak{p}_{v}$ splits in $E$,}\end{cases}

where
\mathfrak{d}_{E}
is the different ideal of
E
. For
\mathfrak{p}_{v}\in\text{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
, with
v\not\in S
(in particular unramified in
E/F
),
set
\mathds{F}^{k}(v):=\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}
. When
k=1
this is the residue field of
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
whose size is
q_{v}:=\#\mathds{F}^{1}(v)
. For any integer
k\geq 1
, set
\mathds{F}^{k}(v)[\upepsilon]:=\mathds{F}^{k}(v)[X]/\langle X^{2}\rangle
to be the infinitesimal deformation
\mathds{F}^{k}(v)
-algebra and set
\mathds{E}^{k}(v):=\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}
. We then have ring isomorphisms

\mathds{E}^{1}(v)\simeq\begin{cases}\text{a quadratic extension of }\mathds{F}^{1}(v)&\text{ if }\left(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{E}}{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}\right)=-1,\\
\mathds{F}^{1}(v)\oplus\mathds{F}^{1}(v)&\text{ if }\left(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{E}}{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}\right)=1.\end{cases}

Here, we summarize a few facts on
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
-orders of
E_{v}
.
The map that sends an ideal
\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c_{v}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
to the order
\mathcal{O}_{v,c_{v}}=\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c_{v}}\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}
induces a bijection between the set of ideals of
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
and the set of
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
-orders in
E_{v}
. These orders are all Gorenstein and local whenever
c_{v}\geq 1
with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{P}_{v,c_{v}}:=\mathfrak{p}_{v}\mathcal{O}_{v,c_{v}-1}
and

\mathcal{O}_{v,c_{v}}/\mathfrak{P}_{v,c_{v}}\simeq\mathds{F}^{1}(v).

When
c_{v}=0
, the order
\mathcal{O}_{v,0}
is only semi-local if
v
splits in
E
, since
\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
.

Let
\Tr\colon\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}\to\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
be the usual trace map
z\mapsto z+\overline{z}
. Let
\alpha_{v}\in\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}^{\times}
be any generator of the rank
1

\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
-module
\ker\Tr
33
33

33

For example, if
v
split then one has a decomposition
\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
, such that
\tau\in\text{Gal}(E/F)
(or just the complex conjugation since we have identified
E
with
\iota_{1}(E)
) acts by swapping the components. Therefore,
\ker\Tr=\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\cdot(1,-1)
and one can take
\alpha_{v}=(1,-1)
, in addition, observe that
\overline{(1,-1)}=-(1,1)
and
(1,-1)^{2}=(1,1)\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
where
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
is embedded diagonally in
\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}
.
, therefore
\overline{\alpha}_{v}=-\alpha_{v},\alpha_{v}^{2}\in\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
and for every
c_{v}\geq 0
we have
\mathcal{O}_{v,c_{v}}=\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c_{v}}\alpha_{v}
.

Lemma 3.10.1
.

Let
c>0
, we have an isomorphism of groups

\dfrac{\mathcal{O}_{v,0}^{\times}}{\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}}\simeq\mathds{E}^{c}(v)^{\times}/\mathds{F}^{c}(v)^{\times}.

Proof.

Consider the following composition of reduction maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}=\mathcal{O}_{v,0}}
E
^k(v)^×
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{E}^{k}(v)^{\times}/\mathds{F}^{k}(v)^{\times}.}

The quotient map of rings
\mathcal{O}_{v,0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{v,0}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0}
induces a surjective homomorphism of groups
\mathcal{O}_{v,0}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,0}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0}\right)^{\times}
with kernel
1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0}
, i.e.

\mathcal{O}_{v,0}^{\times}/1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0}\simeq\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,0}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0}\right)^{\times}.

But since the diagonal image of
\mathcal{O}_{F}\to\mathcal{O}_{v,0}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0}
is precisely
\mathds{F}^{k}(v)
, we deduce that the kernel of the composition following of reduction maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\colon\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}=\mathcal{O}_{v,0}}
E
^k(v)^×
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{E}^{k}(v)^{\times}/\mathds{F}^{k}(v)^{\times}.}

is precisely
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}(1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{v,0})=\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}.
∎

Lemma 3.10.2
.

Let
c\geq k>0
, we have an isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}/\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}^{\times}\simeq\left(\mathds{F}^{k}(v)[\upepsilon]\right)^{\times}\big/\mathds{F}^{k}(v)^{\times}.

Proof.

As in the previous proof, consider the quotient map of rings
\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{v,c}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}
. It induces a surjective homomorphism of groups
\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,c}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\right)^{\times}
with kernel
1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}
, i.e.

\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}/1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\simeq\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,c}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\right)^{\times}.

We may also consider the map of rings

\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}
. It is clearly surjective and induces the isomorphism of groups

\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}^{\times}/\big(1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\big)\simeq\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\right)^{\times}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}/\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}^{\times}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}/(1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c})\big/\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}^{\times}/(1+\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c})

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,c}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\right)^{\times}\big/\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\right)^{\times}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\mathcal{O}_{v,c}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}\right)^{\times}\big/\left(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\right)^{\times}

Recall
\mathds{F}^{k}(v)=\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
and consider the homomorphism of rings

\mathds{F}^{k}(v)[X]\to\mathcal{O}_{v,c}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c}
, given by
X\mapsto(\varpi_{v}^{c}\alpha_{v}\mod\mathfrak{p}_{v}^{k}\mathcal{O}_{v,c})
.
The kernel contains
\langle X^{2}\rangle
(because
c\geq k
) and gives a surjective map between two sets with the same order.
Hence, for any
c\geq k>0
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}/\mathcal{O}_{v,c+k}^{\times}\simeq

\displaystyle\left(\mathds{F}^{k}(v)[\upepsilon]\right)^{\times}\big/\mathds{F}^{k}(v)^{\times}.\qed

In particular,
\mathbb{G}_{v}:=\mathds{E}^{1}(v)^{\times}/\mathds{F}^{1}(v)^{\times}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{v,0}^{\times}/\mathcal{O}_{v,1}^{\times}

is (cyclic if
v
is inert in
E/F
) of order
q_{v}-\left(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{E}}{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}\right)
and
\mathbb{G}_{v}(\upepsilon):={\mathds{F}^{1}(v)[\upepsilon]^{\times}/\mathds{F}^{1}(v)^{\times}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}/\mathcal{O}_{v,c+1}^{\times}
.
Finally, for every
c>0
, we have the following short exact sequence (of abelian groups)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

O

v,1

×

O

v,c

×

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\mathcal{O}_{v,0}^{\times}}{\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}}}

O

v,0

×

O

v,1

×

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1,}

thus

\#\left(\dfrac{\mathcal{O}_{v,0}^{\times}}{\mathcal{O}_{v,c}^{\times}}\right)=|\mathbb{G}_{v}(\upepsilon)|^{c-1}\cdot|\mathbb{G}_{v}|=q_{v}^{c-1}\left(q_{v}-\left(\frac{\mathfrak{d}_{E}}{\mathfrak{p}_{v}}\right)\right).

3.11.

Definition 3.11.1
.

Set

\mathcal{P}:=\left\{\mathfrak{p}\in\text{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{F})\colon\mathfrak{p}\text{ is unramified in }E/F,\mathfrak{p}\not\in S,\mathfrak{p}\nmid\mathfrak{c}_{1},\mathfrak{p}\mathcal{O}_{E}\nmid I_{0}\right\},

.

Denote by

the set of square-free products of ideals in
\mathcal{P}
:

\forall r\geq 1\colon\mathcal{N}_{?}^{r}:=\{\mathfrak{p}_{1}\cdots\mathfrak{p}_{r}\colon\mathfrak{p}_{j}\in\mathcal{P}_{?}\text{ distinct}\}\text{ for }?\in\{\text{in, sp}\}.

So in particular, one has:

Proposition 3.11.2
.

, which says

.

, recall that by (

) and since

, we have an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{E^{\times}\cap\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{\times}\mathfrak{O}_{1}^{\times}}{F^{\times}}}

hence, the extension

is of degree

4.
Proof of distribution relations in split cases

Let
v\in\mathcal{P}_{sp}
. We recall once again that an embedding
\iota_{v}\colon\overline{F}\hookrightarrow\overline{F}_{v}
has been fixed in at the beginning of §
2
. Let
w
the place of
E
above
v
determined by
\iota_{v}
and
\overline{w}
its conjugate. We abuse notation and also write
w
for the place above
w
determined by this choice in any field extension of
E
contained in the fixed algebraic closure
\overline{F}
. Let
\varpi_{v}
be a uniformizer for
F_{v}=E_{w}
,
q_{v}
for the cardinality of the residue field and
p
for the rational prime below
v
.

4.1.
Normalization isomorphism in the split case

We identify the group
{\mathbf{U}}(V)_{/{F_{v}}}\times{\mathbf{U}}(W)_{/{F_{v}}}
with
\GL(V_{w})_{/{F_{v}}}\times\GL(W_{w})_{/{F_{v}}}
as follows: Recall that for any Hermitian
E
-space
\mathcal{V}

{\mathbf{U}}({\mathcal{V}})(F_{v})=\{g\in\GL(\mathcal{V})(E\otimes_{F}F_{v})\colon\psi_{v}(gx,gy)=\psi_{v}(x,y),\ \forall x,y\in\mathcal{V}\otimes_{F}F_{v}\},

where
\psi_{v}=\psi_{F_{v}}
(see footnote
8
in on page
8
). We have

E_{v}=E\otimes_{F}F_{v}=E_{{w}}\oplus E_{\overline{w}}\simeq F_{v}\oplus F_{v},

where, the action of complex conjugation on the left-hand side corresponds to the involution
(s,t)\mapsto(t,s)
on the right-hand side. Thus, one has
\mathcal{V}\otimes_{F}F_{v}=\mathcal{V}_{{w}}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}
,
and

\GL(\mathcal{V})(E\otimes_{F}F_{v})=\GL(\mathcal{V}_{{w}})\times\GL(\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}})\simeq\GL(\mathcal{V})(F_{v})\times\GL(\mathcal{V})(F_{v}).

The hermitian form
\psi_{v}
takes values in
E_{v}=E_{w}\oplus E_{\overline{w}}
, write
\psi_{v}=(\psi_{w},\psi_{\overline{w}})
for its two component. For any
x,y\in\mathcal{V}\otimes_{F}F_{v}=\mathcal{V}_{{w}}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}
, write
x=x_{{w}}+x_{\overline{w}}
and
y=y_{{w}}+y_{\overline{w}}
. Recall that the original hermitian form
\psi
is semi-linear on the right. By definition
\psi_{v}(x,y)=\left(\psi_{w}(x,y),\psi_{\overline{w}}(x,y)\right)
, accordingly we have

\displaystyle\psi_{v}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\psi_{v}\left(x_{{w}},y_{\overline{w}}\right)+\psi_{v}\left(x_{\overline{w}},y_{{w}}\right)+\psi_{v}\left(x_{{w}},y_{{w}}\right)+\psi_{v}\left(x_{\overline{w}},y_{\overline{w}}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\psi_{v}\left(x_{{w}},y_{\overline{w}}\right)+\psi_{v}\left(x_{\overline{w}},y_{{w}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{w}(x_{w},y_{\overline{w}}),\psi_{\overline{w}}(x_{w},y_{\overline{w}})\right)+\left(\psi_{{w}}(x_{\overline{w}},y_{w}),\psi_{\overline{w}}(x_{\overline{w}},y_{w})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{w}(x_{w},y_{\overline{w}}),0\right)+\left(0,\psi_{\overline{w}}(x_{\overline{w}},y_{w})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{w}(x_{w},y_{\overline{w}}),\psi_{\overline{w}}(x_{\overline{w}},y_{w})\right)

For (1), we have used the fact that
\psi_{v}=0\text{ on }\mathcal{V}_{w}\times\mathcal{V}_{w}\text{ and }\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}
. To show this, let
x,y
be any elements in
\mathcal{V}_{w}
then
x=(1,0)x
where
(1,0)\in F_{v}\oplus F_{v}
, so
\psi_{v}(x,y)=\psi_{v}((1,0)x,(1,0)y)=(1,0){(1,0)}^{\tau}\psi_{v}(x,y)=(0,0)\psi_{v}(x,y)=0
. We show similarly the annihilation of
\psi_{v}=0
on
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}
. Accordingly, we have

i.

\psi_{w}=0
on
\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}\times\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}_{w}
and induces a perfect pairing
{\psi_{w}\colon\mathcal{V}_{{w}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}\to F_{v}}
.

ii.

\psi_{\overline{w}}=0
on
\mathcal{V}_{{w}}\times\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}
and induces a perfect pairing
{\psi_{\overline{w}}\colon\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}\times\mathcal{V}_{{w}}\to F_{v}}
.

iii.

\psi_{\overline{w}}(x_{\overline{w}},y_{w})=\psi_{{w}}(y_{w},x_{\overline{w}})
, since
\psi_{v}(x,y)=\tau({\psi_{v}(y,x)})
.

Let
g=(g_{1},g_{2})\in\GL(\mathcal{V}_{{w}})\times\GL(\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}})

\displaystyle\psi_{v}((gx,gy))

\displaystyle=\psi_{v}\left((g_{1},g_{2})(x_{w}+x_{\overline{w}}),(g_{1},g_{2})(y_{w}+y_{\overline{w}})\right)

\displaystyle=\psi_{v}\left(g_{1}x_{w}+g_{2}x_{\overline{w}}x,g_{1}y_{w}+g_{2}y_{\overline{w}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{w}(g_{1}x_{w},g_{2}y_{\overline{w}}),\psi_{\overline{w}}(g_{2}x_{\overline{w}},g_{1}y_{w})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\psi_{w}(g_{1}x_{w},g_{2}y_{\overline{w}}),\psi_{{w}}(g_{1}y_{w},g_{2}x_{\overline{w}})\right)

Now, if
g=(g_{1},g_{2})
is in
\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}(F_{v})\subset\GL(\mathcal{V}_{{w}})\times\GL(\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}})
, then

\psi_{v}((gx,gy))=\psi_{v}(x,y)=\left(\psi_{w}(x_{{w}},y_{\overline{w}}),\psi_{w}(y_{{w}},x_{{\overline{w}}})\right).

Hence,
g=(g_{1},g_{2})\in\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{\mathcal{V}}(F_{v})
if and only if
\psi_{w}(g_{1}x_{{w}},g_{2}y_{\overline{w}})=\psi_{w}(x_{{w}},y_{\overline{w}})
for all
x_{{w}}\in\mathcal{V}_{{w}}
and
y_{\overline{w}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\overline{w}}
. The discussion above shows that the projection
g=(g_{1},g_{2})\mapsto g_{1}
defines an isomorphism
\underline{\mathbf{U}}(\mathcal{V})(F_{v})\simeq\GL(\mathcal{V}_{{w}})
that is actually defined over
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
. Now, since
{w}
is the place of
E
corresponding to the fixed embedding
\iota_{v}\colon\overline{F}\hookrightarrow\overline{F}_{v}
, there is no ambiguity in writing
\mathcal{V}_{{w}}
as
\mathcal{V}_{v}
and viewing it as a vector space over
F_{v}=E_{{w}}
. Therefore, we get the desired identifications.

Remark 4.1.1
.

If we follow Remark
3.6.1
and pick up an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-lattice
\mathcal{L}_{W}
in
W
,
\mathcal{L}_{D}
in
D
and
\mathcal{L}_{V}=\mathcal{L}_{W}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{D}
in
V
, then working with Hermitian spaces over
\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
gives an identification:

\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{V,\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}}\simeq\GL_{n+1,\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\underline{\mathbf{U}}_{W,\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}}\simeq\GL_{n,\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}}

using the previous formulas throughout. We will retain this notation in §
4
.

As we have pointed out in §
3.10
, under the identification
E_{v}\simeq F_{v}\oplus F_{v}
, the maximal order
\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}
of the étale algebra
E_{v}
identifies with
\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{E_{w}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{E_{\overline{w}}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{F_{\tau}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{F_{\tau}}
. The groups

U_{v}^{1}(c)=\underline{\nu}({\mathcal{O}_{v,c}}^{\times})=\underline{\nu}({\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}+\varpi_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{E_{v}}}^{\times})=\{(z,z^{-1})\colon z\in 1+\varpi_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}\}\text{ for }c\in\mathds{N},

defined in §
3.7
, yield the decreasing filtration
(H_{c}:=\det^{-1}(U_{v}^{1}(c)))_{c\in\mathds{N}}
, on
\mathbf{H}(F_{v})
. The
E
determinant map on
{\mathbf{U}}({\star})
becomes under the isomorphism
{\mathbf{U}}(V)_{/F_{v}}\simeq\GL(\star)_{/F_{v}}
the usual determinant map and the groups
U_{v}^{1}(c)
become
1+\varpi^{c}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
.

By abuse of notation, we will also use the notation
H_{c}
for the corresponding subgroup
{\det}^{-1}(1+\varpi_{v}^{c}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})\subset\GL_{n}(F_{v})
via the isomorphism
\mathbf{H}(F_{v})\simeq\GL_{n}(F_{v})
fixed above.

4.2.
Action of Frobenii on unramified special cycles

Let
g\in\mathbf{G}(\mathds{A}_{f})
.
Assume that the field of definition of the cycle
\mathfrak{z}_{g}
is unramified at
v
.
The description of the Galois action on special cycles in §
3.4
using
\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})
, together with the discussion in §
4.1
imply that the action of
\Fr_{w}\in\text{Gal}(E_{w}^{un}/E_{w})
(the geometric Frobenius) on the cycle

is given by

\Fr_{w}\cdot\mathfrak{z}_{g}=\mathfrak{z}_{\Delta_{v}(\textbf{Frob}_{v})\cdot g},

where,

is any matrix verifying

\text{ord}_{{F_{v}}}(\det(\textbf{Frob}_{v}))=1,\text{ e.g.}\;\textbf{Frob}_{v}=\diag(\varpi_{v},\cdots,1),

and
\Delta_{v}
is the composition of the following natural embeddings:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{v}\colon\mathbf{U}(W)(F_{v})}
U
(W)(

_F,f)
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{U}(V)(\mathds{A}_{F,f})\times\mathbf{U}(W)(\mathds{A}_{F,f})\simeq\mathbf{G}(\mathds{A}_{f}).}

4.3.2.
Dual group

The complex dual of
\GL_{n+1,F_{v}}\times\GL_{n,F_{v}}
is

\widehat{\GL}_{n+1,F_{v}}\times\widehat{\GL}_{n,F_{v}}=\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C}).

Let
(\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{w},\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
be the standard Borel pair dual to
({\mathbf{B}}_{w},{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
, that is the upper triangular matrices and its maximal torus of diagonal matrices. For convenience, we write
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{w}=\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{v,1}\times\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{v,2}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}=\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{v,1}\times\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{v,2}
and
W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}):=W(\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C}),\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
for the Weyl group. The Galois group
\text{Gal}(F_{v}^{un}/F_{v})
acts trivially on dual of
{\GL}_{n+1,F_{v}}\times{\GL}_{n,F_{v}}
, since it a split group, i.e.

{}^{L}({\GL}_{n+1,F_{v}}\times{\GL}_{n,F_{v}})=\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})\times\text{Gal}(F_{v}^{un}/F_{v}).

4.3.3.
The character
\widehat{\mu}_{h,v}

Under the identification
X^{*}(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})=X_{*}({\mathbf{T}}_{w})
, the Weyl orbit of
\mu_{h,v}
corresponds to a Weyl
W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
-orbit of characters of
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
. There is a unique
\widehat{\mu}_{h,v}\in X^{*}(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
in this Weyl orbit that is dominant with respect to the Borel subgroup
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{w}
and it is explicitly given on
\mathds{C}
-points by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mu}_{h,v}\colon{\mathbf{T}}_{w}(\mathds{C})}
C
,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\diag(z_{1},\cdots,z_{n+1}),\diag(z_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,z_{n}^{\prime})\right)}
z_1 z_1’ .

4.3.4.
The representation

The representation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r\colon\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})}
GL_n(n+1),

we are interested in is the irreducible representation whose highest weight relative to the Borel pair
(\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{w},\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
is
\widehat{\mu}_{h,v}

[
BR94
, §5.1]
. Let
r_{?}
be the standard
?
-dimensional representation of
\GL_{?}(\mathds{C})
, then
r
is the representation on the
2
-fold tensor product
r_{n+1}\otimes r_{n}
defined as follows, for any
g=(g_{1},g_{2})\in\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})
,

r(g)=r_{n+1}(g_{1})\otimes r_{n}(g_{2}).

Finally, extend
r
to a representation of
{}^{L}(\GL_{n+1,F_{v}}\times\GL_{n,F_{v}})
(also called
r
) by letting the Galois group act trivially everywhere.

4.3.5.
The Hecke polynomial

Definition.
Following
[
Bou21b
, Definition
]
, we attach to the pair
({\underline{\mathbf{G}}}_{F_{v}},[\mu_{h,v}])
the polynomial

H_{w}(z)=\det\left(z-q_{v}^{{\langle\mu_{h,v},\rho_{v}\rangle}}r(g)\right)\in\mathds{C}[\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})][z],

where,
\rho_{v}
is the halfsum of all positive roots of
({\mathbf{B}}_{w},{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
, thus
\langle\mu_{h,v},\rho_{v}\rangle={2n-1}
.
The ring of coefficients of this polynomial, is the ring of class functions on
\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})
.
A class function on
\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})
restricts to a Weyl-invariant function on
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
. Conversely, by a classical argument of Chevalley, the subalgebra of Weyl-invariants functions on
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
consists precisely of functions which arise from class functions on
\GL_{n+1}(\mathds{C})\times\GL_{n}(\mathds{C})
. Therefore, one can identify
H_{w}(z)
with a polynomial in
\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}]^{W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})}[t]
, which, abusing notation, we continue to denote by
H_{w}
:

H_{w}(t)=\det\left(z-q_{v}^{{\langle\mu_{h,v},\rho_{v}\rangle}}r_{|\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}}(t)\right)\in\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}][t].

Let
t=\left(\diag{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n+1})},\diag{(y_{1},\cdots,y_{n})}\right)\in\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
, the polynomial then identifies with

H_{w}(t)=\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(z-q_{v}^{{2n-1}}x_{i}y_{j}\right)\in\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}][t].

Here,
x_{i}y_{j}
denotes the function
x_{i}y_{j}\colon\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}\rightarrow\mathds{C}
defined by

(\diag{(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n+1})},\diag{(y_{1},\cdots,y_{n})})\mapsto x_{i}y_{j}.

Now, using the identification
36
36

36

This identification is obtained as follows: On the one hand, by definition of the dual torus, the ring of algebraic functions on
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}
are precisely the elements of
\mathds{C}[X_{*}(\mathbf{T}_{w})]
. On the other hand, sending any
F_{v}
-algebraic cocharacter
\chi\colon\mathds{G}_{m,{F}_{v}}\rightarrow\mathbf{T}_{w}
to
1_{\chi(\varpi_{v})\mathbf{T}_{w}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})}
, yields an identification
\mathds{C}[X_{*}(\mathbf{T}_{w})]\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\mathds{C}}(\mathbf{T}_{w}(F_{v})\sslash\mathbf{T}_{w}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}))
.

\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}]\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\mathds{C}}({\mathbf{T}}_{w}(F_{v})\sslash{\mathbf{T}}_{w}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}))
, this function corresponds to the element
\mathbf{1}_{({g_{i}},{h_{j}})T_{c}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathds{C}}({{\mathbf{T}_{w}}}(F_{v})\sslash{{\mathbf{T}_{w}}}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}))
where

g_{i}=\diag{(\underbrace{1,\dots 1}_{i-1},\varpi_{v},1\dots,1)}\text{ and }h_{j}=\diag(\underbrace{1,\dots 1}_{j-1},\varpi_{v},1,\dots,1).

Since the Weyl group
W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})
permutes the
x_{i}
’s and
y_{j}
’s, it is clear that
H_{w}(t)\in\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}]^{W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})}[z]
. Subsequently, using the Satake isomorphism

\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}]^{W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\mathds{C}}(\mathbf{T}_{w}(F_{v})\sslash\mathbf{T}_{w}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}))^{W(\mathbf{T}_{w})}\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\mathds{C}}(\underline{\mathbf{G}}(F_{v})\sslash\underline{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})),

we may also view
H_{w}
as a polynomial with coefficients in the local spherical Hecke algebra

\mathcal{H}(\GL_{n+1}(F_{v})\sslash\GL_{n+1}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}),\mathds{Z}[q_{v}^{\pm 1/2}])\times\mathcal{H}(\GL_{n}(F_{v})\sslash\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}),\mathds{Z}[q_{v}^{\pm 1/2}]).

Explicit polynomial.
In the remaining part of this section, we will give an explicit formulation for the Hecke polynomial with coefficients in the spherical local Hecke algebra, i.e. we will invert the Satake isomorphism. Let us begin by rewriting this polynomial in a more suitable form:

\displaystyle H_{w}(t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\left(z-q_{v}^{{2n-1}}x_{i}y_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}(\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}(-1)^{k}q_{v}^{{(2n-1)k}}X_{k}{y_{j}^{k}}z^{n+1-k})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}\big(\sum_{(a_{i})\in p_{n}(k)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{a_{i}}q_{v}^{{2n-1}a_{i}}X_{a_{i}}y_{i}^{a_{i}}\big)z^{n(n+1)-k}

where,
p_{n}(k):=\{(a_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}\in\mathds{N}\colon\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}=k,0\leq a_{i}\leq n+1\}
and
X_{?}
is the symmetric monomial associated to the monome
x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{?}
, for
1\leq?\leq n+1
. Here, the symmetric permutation group
S_{n}
acts on the set of partitions
p_{n}(j)
and yields

\displaystyle H_{w}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}\big(\sum_{(a_{i})\in p_{n}(k)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{a_{i}}q_{v}^{(2n-1)a_{i}}X_{a_{i}}y_{i}^{a_{i}}\big)z^{n(n+1)-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}\big(\sum_{(a_{i})\in p_{n}(k)/S_{n}}(-1)^{\sum a_{i}}q_{v}^{{2n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}X_{a_{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}^{a_{i}})\big)z^{n(n+1)-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}(-1)^{k}q_{v}^{{k(2n-1)}}\big(\sum_{(a_{i})\in p_{n}(k)/S_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}X_{a_{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}^{a_{i}})\big)z^{n(n+1)-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}(-1)^{k}q_{v}^{{k(2n-1)}}\big(\sum_{(a_{i})\in p_{n}(k)/S_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}X_{a_{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}Y^{(a_{i})}\big)z^{n(n+1)-k}

where,
Y^{(?)}
denotes the power sum symmetric monomial
\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{?}
, for
?\geq 1
. Set
Y_{?}
for the symmetric monomial associated to the monome
y_{1}y_{2}\cdots y_{?}
, for
1\leq?\leq n
. For any
k\geq 1
, the Newton–Girard formula says:

Y^{(k)}=\begin{cases}(-1)^{k}kY_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{k-1+i}Y_{k-i}Y^{(i)}&\text{ if }1\leq k\leq n,\\
\sum_{i=k-n}^{k-1}(-1)^{k-1+i}Y_{k-i}Y^{(i)}&\text{ if }n<k.\end{cases}

There exists then, a polynomial in
n
variables

Q_{k}(z_{1},\cdots,z_{n})\in\mathds{Z}[z_{1},\cdots,z_{n}]
such that the power sum
Y^{(k)}
is given by

Y^{(k)}=Q_{k}(Y_{1},\dots,Y_{n}).

Set
T_{k,\star}
for the Hecke operator
{\GL_{\star}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})\cdot\diag(\underbrace{\varpi_{v},\cdots,\varpi_{v}}_{k},1,\cdots,1)\cdot\GL_{\star}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})}
, for
\star\in\{V,W\}
.
The Satake isomorphism
\mathds{C}[\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w}]^{W(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{w})}\simeq{\mathcal{H}_{\mathds{C}}(\underline{\mathbf{G}}(F_{v})\sslash\underline{\mathbf{G}}(\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}))}
yields the following identifications

(4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{k},(1\leq k\leq n+1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{v}^{-\frac{(n+1-k)k}{2}}T_{k,V},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{k},(1\leq k\leq n)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{v}^{-\frac{(n-k)k}{2}}T_{k,W}}

Indeed, for any fixed integer
?\geq 1
and every integer
1\leq i\leq?
we consider the following minuscule
37
37

37

i.e. the representation
\text{Ad}\circ\lambda_{i}
of
\mathds{G}_{m}
on
\text{Lie}(\GL_{?})
has no weights other than
1,0,-1
.
cocharcater
\lambda_{i}\colon\mathds{G}_{m,F}\to\GL_{?}
given on
F_{v}
points by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda_{i}\colon F_{v}^{\times}}
GL_?(F),
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\mapsto\diag(\underbrace{t,\cdots,t}_{i-\text{tuple}},1\cdots,1).}

The above identification is an immediate application of Proposition
[
Bou21b
, Proposition
]
, where we also use the fact that the Modulus function for
\GL_{\star}
with respect to the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matirces
B_{?}
is given by
\delta_{B_{?}}(\diag(\varpi^{a_{i}}))=q_{v}^{-\sum_{j=1}^{?}(?+1-2j)a_{j}}
, for any diagonal matrix

.

Consequently we can now write the Hecke polynomial with coefficients in the spherical local Hecke algebra:

\displaystyle H_{w}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n(n+1)}(-1)^{k}q_{v}^{\frac{3k(n-1)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{2}}{2}}\big(\sum_{(a_{i})\in p_{n}(k)/S_{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}T_{a_{i},V}\otimes\prod_{i=1}^{n}T_{W}^{(a_{i})}\big)z^{n(n+1)-k}

where,
T_{W}^{(a_{i})}:=Q_{a_{i}}(q_{v}^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}T_{1,W},\dots,q_{v}^{-\frac{(n-j)j}{2}}T_{j,W},\dots,T_{n,W})
.

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

We would like to describe the quotient
H\backslash G/\penalty K
where
K=\mathbf{G}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
. Set
K_{1}=\GL_{n+1}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
and
K_{2}=\GL_{n}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
. For every
(g_{1},g_{2})\in G
, we have
H(g_{1},g_{2})=H(\iota(g_{2})^{-1}g_{1},1)\in H\backslash G
. This induces a bijection
H\backslash G\simeq\GL_{n+1}(F)
, given by
H(g_{1},g_{2})\mapsto\iota(g_{2})^{-1}g_{1}
. The natural right action of
G
on the quotient space
H\backslash G
corresponds to the following right action on
\GL_{n+1}(F)
:

\forall(g_{1},g_{2})\in G,\,\forall g\in\GL_{n+1}(F),\quad g\cdot(g_{1},g_{2})=\iota(g_{2})^{-1}g\,g_{1}\in\GL_{n+1}(F).

This isomorphism of right
G
-spaces, induces the following bijection

\displaystyle H\backslash G/\penalty K

\displaystyle\simeq\{g\cdot K\colon g\in\GL_{n+1}(F)\}

\displaystyle\simeq\{\iota(K_{2})^{-1}g\,K_{1}\colon g\in\GL_{n+1}(F)\}

\displaystyle\simeq\iota(K_{2})\backslash\GL_{n+1}(F)/\penalty K_{1}.

Let
\mathbf{B}_{1}\subset\GL_{n+1}
(resp.
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}\subset\GL_{n}
) be the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matrices. We denote their respective opposite Borel subgroups by
{\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{1}}
and
{\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
. Consider the Borel subgroup
{\mathbf{B}^{\sim}}:=\mathbf{B}_{1}\times{\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}\subset\mathbf{G}
. Let
\mathbf{U}_{1}
(resp
\mathbf{U}_{2}
) be the unipotent radical of
\mathbf{B}_{1}
(resp.
\mathbf{B}_{2}
) and
\mathbf{T}=\mathbf{T}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}_{2}\subset{\mathbf{B}^{\sim}}
be the split maximal torus where
\mathbf{T}_{1}
(resp.
\mathbf{T}_{2}
) is the split maximal diagonal torus of
\GL_{n+1}
(resp.
\GL_{n}
).
The set of
{\mathbf{B}^{\sim}}
-antidominant diagonals is
{T^{\sim}}={T_{1}^{-}}\times{T_{2}^{+}}
, where

{T_{1}^{-}}=\left\{\diag(\varpi^{a_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n+1},\colon a_{i}\in\mathds{Z}\text{ such that }a_{1}\geq\dots\geq a_{n+1}\right\}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{F}),

{T_{2}^{+}}=\left\{\diag(\varpi^{b_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n},\colon b_{i}\in\mathds{Z}\text{ such that }b_{1}\leq\dots\leq b_{n}\right\}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F}).

Proposition 4.4.1
.

Every class in
H\backslash G/\penalty K
admits a representative of the form

\left(\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&1\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&1\\
&&&\varpi^{c}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{b_{1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\varpi^{b_{n}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\in G,

for some
c\in\mathds{Z}
and
\left(\diag(\varpi^{a_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n+1},\diag(\varpi^{b_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n}\right)\in{T^{\sim}}
(with
a_{n+1}:=0
).

Proof.

Let us first prove that the set

\left\{\varpi^{c}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&\varpi^{b_{1}}\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&\varpi^{b_{n}}\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}\colon c\in\mathds{Z},a_{n}\geq b_{n}\text{ and }k<l\Rightarrow a_{k}-a_{l}\geq b_{k}-b_{l}\geq 0\right\},

is a set of class representatives for the quotient

\iota(K_{2})\backslash\GL_{n+1}(F)/\penalty K_{1}.

Observe that
\iota(\GL_{n})\times\chi_{1,n+1}(\mathds{G}_{m})
is a Levi factor of the parabolic subgroup:

\mathbf{P}:=\begin{pmatrix}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

&\GL_{n}&&\vdots\\

&&&\mathds{G}_{m}\end{pmatrix}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

&&&\mathds{G}_{m}\end{pmatrix}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&&*\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix},

where, the second factor of the Levi decomposition above is the unipotent radical
\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}}
of
\mathbf{P}
. Therefore, every class in
\iota(K_{2})\backslash\GL_{n+1}(F)/\penalty K_{1}
has a representatives of the form
\varpi^{c}\iota(g)u
for some
c\in\mathds{Z}
,
g\in\GL_{n}(F)
and
u\in\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}}(F)
. By the Cartan decomposition for
\GL_{n}(F)
, we know there exist
k,k^{\prime}\in K_{2}
such that
k^{\prime}gk=\diag(\varpi^{a_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n}
with
a_{1}\geq\dots\geq a_{n}
. Hence

\iota(K_{2})\varpi^{c}guK_{1}=\iota(K_{2})\varpi^{c}\iota(k^{\prime}gk)\iota(k)^{-1}u\,\iota(k)K_{1}.

Since
\iota(k)^{-1}u\,\iota(k)\in\mathbf{U}_{\mathbf{P}}(F)
, then each class in
\iota(K_{2})\backslash\GL_{n+1}(F)/\penalty K_{1}
has a representative of the form

\varpi^{c}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&\varpi^{b_{1}}d_{1}\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&\varpi^{b_{n}}d_{n}\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}

For some
c,a_{k},b_{k}\in\mathds{Z}
such that
k<l\Rightarrow a_{k}-a_{l}\geq 0
and,
d_{k}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
.

•

\underline{d_{k}\neq 0}:
Suppose some of the
d_{k}
’s is zero. Using a unipotent matrix from the right we can replace
\text{Column}_{n+1}
by
\text{Column}_{n+1}+\text{Column}_{k}
. Thus we get a new matrix with
d_{k}=1
and
b_{k}
is equal to
a_{k}
.

•

\underline{d_{k}=1}:
Suppose then that all
d_{k}
’s are non zero. Conjugation by the matrix
\diag(d_{1},\dots,d_{n},1)\in\iota(K_{2})
shows that one can ignore the
d_{k}
’s:

\varpi^{c}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&\varpi^{b_{1}}\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&\varpi^{b_{n}}\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}

•

\underline{a_{n}\geq b_{n}}:
If we have
a_{n}<b_{n}
, we may change
b_{n}
to
a_{n}
by replacing the
\text{Column}_{n+1}
by
\text{Column}_{n+1}+\text{Column}_{n}
.

•

(*)
\underline{b_{1}\geq\dots\geq b_{n}}:
Let
k<l
and suppose that
b_{k}<b_{l}
.
We can find a matrix in
\iota(K_{2})
taking
\text{Row}_{l}
to
\text{Row}_{l}+\text{Row}_{k}
, thus we get in the
k^{th}
row
\varpi^{b_{k}}+\varpi^{b_{l}}=\varpi^{b_{k}}(1+\varpi^{b_{l}-b_{k}})
. We kill the invertible element
1+\varpi^{b_{k}-b_{l}}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}
by adjoint action of a diagonal matrix in
\iota(K_{2})
having
(1+\varpi^{b_{l}-b_{k}})^{-1}
in the
l^{th}
component. Now we kill the element that appears in the
(l,k)
position of the new matrix: use the action of
K_{1}
on columns to take
\text{Column}_{k}
to
\text{Column}_{k}-\varpi^{a_{k}-a_{l}}(1+\varpi^{b_{l}-b_{k}})^{-1}\text{Column}_{l}
. We thus have changed the matrix by replacing -only- the old
b_{l}
by
b_{k}
.

•

\underline{a_{k}-a_{l}\geq b_{k}-b_{l}}:
Fix a
k\leq n
. Suppose that for some
l>k
we have
c_{k,l}:=(b_{k}-b_{l})-(a_{k}-a_{l})>0
. Let
l^{\prime}
be such that
c_{k,l^{\prime}}=\max_{l\geq k}c_{k,l}
. Take
\text{Row}_{k}
to

\text{Row}_{k}+\varpi^{a_{k}-a_{l^{\prime}}}\text{Row}_{l^{\prime}}=\text{Row}_{k}+\varpi^{b_{k}-b_{l^{\prime}}-c_{k,l^{\prime}}}\text{Row}_{l^{\prime}},\quad(a_{k}-a_{l^{\prime}}\geq 0).

Note that for every
l\geq k
we have

(b_{k}-c_{k,l^{\prime}}-b_{l})-(a_{k}-a_{l})\leq 0.

We clear the component appearing in the
(k,l)
position by replacing
\text{Column}_{l^{\prime}}
by
\text{Column}_{l^{\prime}}-\text{Column}_{k}
. Therefore, we may
38
38

38

The last coefficient of the
k
-th row is
\varpi^{b}_{k}+\varpi^{b_{l^{\prime}}+a_{k}-a_{l^{\prime}}}

which is not of the form
\varpi^{b^{\prime}_{k}}
. Writing this as
\varpi^{b_{l^{\prime}}+a_{k}-a_{l^{\prime}}}u
with
u
invertible, one can then use yet another conjugation to get it to
\varpi^{b^{\prime}_{k}}
with
b^{\prime}_{k}=b_{l^{\prime}}+a_{k}-a_{l^{\prime}}=b_{k}-c_{k,l^{\prime}}
.
replace in the old matrix (only)
b_{k}
by
b_{k}-c_{k,l^{\prime}}
. By doing so, we do not alter the previous required inequalities.

Suppose there is an
l\geq k
such that the new
b_{k}
is
<b_{l}
. Using the step (*) above, we saw that we can replace
b_{l}
by
b_{k}
. By doing so, we do not alter the required inequality
(b_{k}-b_{l})-(a_{k}-a_{l})\leq-(a_{k}-a_{l})\leq 0
.

Now, every class
\iota(K_{2})\varpi^{c}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&\varpi^{b_{1}}\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&\varpi^{b_{n}}\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}K_{1}
corresponds to

H\left(\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}-b_{1}}&&&1\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}-b_{n}}&1\\
&&&\varpi^{c}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{-b_{1}-c}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\varpi^{-b_{n}-c}\end{pmatrix}\right)K\in H\backslash G/K,

such that
-b_{1}\leq\dots\leq-b_{n}
and
a_{1}-b_{1}\geq\dots\geq a_{n}-b_{n}\geq 0
. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma 4.4.2
.

For
\star\in\{1,2\}
, let

g_{\star}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1,\star}}&&&1\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n,\star}}&1\\
&&&\varpi^{c_{\star}}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{b_{1,\star}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\varpi^{b_{n,\star}}\end{pmatrix}\right),

for some
a_{k,\star},b_{k,\star},c_{\star}\in\mathds{Z}
such that for
\star\in\{1,2\}
the sequences
(a_{k,\star})_{k}
is non-increasing and
(b_{k,\star})_{k}
is non-decreasing.
If
Hg_{1}K=Hg_{2}K
then
c_{1}=c_{2}
and
{a_{k,1}-b_{k,1}=a_{k,2}-b_{k,2}}
for each
1\leq k\leq n
.

Proof.

g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are the same in
H\backslash G/\penalty K
. This is true if and only if there exists
k_{2}\in K_{2}
and
k_{1}\in K_{1}
such that

&&\varpi^{a_{n,1}-b_{n,1}}&\varpi^{-b_{n,1}}\\
&&&\varpi^{c_{1}}\end{pmatrix}k_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1,2}-b_{1,2}}&&&\varpi^{-b_{1,2}}\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n,2}-b_{n,2}}&\varpi^{-b_{n,2}}\\
&&&\varpi^{c_{2}}\end{pmatrix}

, i.e.

Here,

belongs to

and

. The equality above implies
\varpi^{c_{2}}=u_{n+1}\varpi^{c_{1}}
(i.e.,
c_{1}=c_{2}
) and

k_{2}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1,1}-b_{1,1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\

&\ddots&\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n,2}-b_{n,2}}\\
\end{pmatrix}

Since both diagonal matrices are
{\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
-antidominant and represent the same class in
K_{2}\backslash\GL_{n}(F)/K_{2}
they must be equal.
∎

Lemma 4.4.3
.

For any integers
c,a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots b_{n}
, consider the matrix

g=\left(\varpi^{c}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&1\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&1\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{b_{1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\varpi^{b_{n}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\in G.

The stabilizer
\Stab_{H}(gK)
is equal to

\begin{split}\big\{\Delta((h_{ij}))\in H\colon h_{ii}\in\mathcal{O}_{F},&h_{ij}\in\varpi^{\max\{a_{i}-a_{j},b_{i}-b_{j}\}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\forall i\neq j,\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\in 1+\varpi^{a_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{F},1\leq\forall i\leq n\big\}.\end{split}

Proof.

Set
\diag(a)=\diag(\varpi^{a_{1}},\dots,\varpi^{a_{n}},1)
and similarly
\diag(b)=\diag(\varpi^{b_{1}},\dots,\varpi^{b_{n}})
. A matrix
h=(h_{ij})\in\GL_{n}(F)
verifies
\Delta(h)\in\Stab_{H}(gK)
by definition if and only if

\begin{cases}\diag(-a)u_{0}^{-1}\iota(h)u_{0}\diag(a)K_{1}&=K_{1}\\
\diag(-b)h\diag(b)K_{2}&=K_{2},\end{cases}

which is equivalent to

\begin{split}\begin{pmatrix}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}&&&\varpi^{-a_{1}}(-1+\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{1j})\\
&\,\diag(-a)h\diag(a)\,&&\vdots\\
&&\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.77779pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

&&&1\end{pmatrix}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@fill@opacity{0.5}\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill-opacity} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.2 4.89 L -2.81 4.89 C -5.87 4.89 -8.35 2.41 -8.35 -0.65 L -8.35 0.65 C -8.35 -2.41 -5.87 -4.89 -2.81 -4.89 L 3.2 -4.89 C 6.25 -4.89 8.73 -2.41 8.73 0.65 L 8.73 -0.65 C 8.73 2.41 6.25 4.89 3.2 4.89 Z M -8.35 -4.89}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.033pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\in K_{1}\text{ and }\diag(-b)h\diag(b)\in K_{2}.\end{split}

Therefore
h\in\diag(a)K_{2}\diag(-a)\cap\diag(b)K_{2}\diag(-b)
and for all
1\leq i\leq n
we must have
\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\in 1+\varpi^{a_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
. This proves that
\Stab_{H}(gK)
is equal to

\displaystyle\{\Delta((h_{ij}))\in H\colon h_{ii}\in\mathcal{O}_{F},\,h_{ij}\in\varpi^{c_{ij}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\text{ and }\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{ij}\in 1+\varpi^{a_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{F},1\leq\forall i\leq n\}.

where,
c_{ij}:=\max\{a_{i}-a_{j},b_{i}-b_{j}\}
for
i\neq j
.
∎

In the following proposition we compute the determinant of
H
-stabilizers of cosets in
G/K
.

Proposition 4.4.4
.

Let
c,a_{1},\dots,a_{n},b_{1},\dots b_{n}
be any integers and consider the element

g=\left(\varpi^{c}\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{a_{1}}&&&1\\
&\ddots&&\vdots\\
&&\varpi^{a_{n}}&1\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\varpi^{b_{1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\varpi^{b_{n}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\in G.

Set
c_{ij}:=\max\{a_{i}-a_{j},b_{i}-b_{j}\}
if
i\neq j
. We then have
\det(\Stab_{H}(gK))=

\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times},\text{ if there exists $i$ such that }a_{i}\leq 0,\\
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times},\text{ if there exists a pair $(i,j)$ such that $i\neq j$ and }c_{ij}\leq 0,\\
1+\varpi^{\min(\{a_{i}:1\leq i\leq n\}\cup\{c_{ij}:1\leq i\neq j\leq n\})}\mathcal{O}_{F}\text{ if }c_{i,j}>0\text{ for all $i\neq j$ and }a_{i}>0.\end{cases}

Proof.

For the first two cases, for each
t\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}
one can give explicitly an element
g_{t}\in H
stabilizing
gK
with determinant
t
:

•

Let
i
be an index for which
a_{i}\leq 0
. One can take
g_{t}=\diag(1,\dots,1,t,1,\dots,1)\in K_{2}
, with
t
in the
i^{th}
position.

•

Let
i
and
j
be indices, for which
c_{ij}\leq 0
. One can take
g_{t}
to be the matrix with
t
in the
i^{th}
diagonal component,
1
’s elsewhere in the diagonal,
1-t
in the
ij
component and zeros everywhere else.

Suppose now, that for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
we have
c_{ij}>0
and,
a_{i}>0
. In this case a matrix
h=(h_{ij})
belongs to
\Stab_{H}(gK)
only if

h_{ij}\in\varpi^{c_{ij}}\mathcal{O}_{F},\forall 1\leq i\neq j\leq n\text{ and }h_{ii}\in 1+\varpi^{\min\{a_{i},c_{ij}\colon 1\leq j\leq n,j\neq i\}}\mathcal{O}_{F},\forall i\leq n.

Therefore, for all
h=(h_{ij})\in\Stab_{H}(gK)
we have
\det(h)\in 1+\varpi^{\min\{a_{i},c_{ij}\colon 1\leq i\neq j\leq n\}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
, i.e.

\det(\Stab_{H}(gK))\subset 1+\varpi^{\min\{a_{i},c_{ij}\colon 1\leq i\neq j\leq n\}}\mathcal{O}_{F}.

The reverse inclusion is as follows: If
\min\{a_{i},c_{ij}\colon 1\leq i\neq j\leq n\}=a_{k}
for some
k\leq n
, then consider for every
t\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
the matrix

h_{t}=\diag(1,\dots,1+\varpi^{a_{k}}t,\dots,1)\in\GL_{n}(F)
. If now
\min\{a_{i},c_{ij}\colon 1\leq i\neq j\leq n\}=c_{kl}
for some
1\leq k\neq l\leq n
, then consider for every
t\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
the matrix
h_{t}
having
1+\varpi^{c_{kl}}t
in the
k^{th}
diagonal component,
1
’s elsewhere in the diagonal,
-\varpi^{c_{kl}}t
in the
kl
component and zeros everywhere else. In both cases, for every
t\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
, we have
h_{t}\in\Stab_{H}(gK)
and
\det(h_{t})=1+\varpi^{\min\{a_{i},c_{ij}\colon 1\leq i\neq j\leq n\}}t
, which shows the reverse inclusion.
∎

4.5.
Local horizontal relation

We prove a local horizontal distribution relation (Corollary
4.5.4
) using a local congruence relation (Theorem
4.5.2
).

Notation

Let
\mathbf{B}={\mathbf{B}}_{1}\times\mathbf{B}_{2}\subset\mathbf{G}
be the Borel subgroup with unipotent radical
{\mathbf{U}}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times{\mathbf{U}_{2}}
. The set of
{\mathbf{B}}
-antidominant diagonals is
{T^{-}}={T_{1}^{-}}\times{T_{2}^{-}}
, where

{T_{1}^{-}}=\left\{\diag(\varpi^{a_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n+1},\colon a_{i}\in\mathds{Z}\text{ such that }a_{1}\geq\dots\geq a_{n+1}\right\}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{F}),

{T_{2}^{-}}=\left\{\diag(\varpi^{b_{k}})_{1\leq k\leq n},\colon b_{i}\in\mathds{Z}\text{ such that }b_{1}\geq\dots\geq b_{n}\right\}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F}).

Consider the following Iwahori subgroup

I={I}_{1}\times I_{2}=\{g\in K\colon(g\mod\varpi)\in B\}.

Let
{\mu}=\Delta\circ\mu_{2}\in T
be the cocharacter coming from the Shimura variety and denoted by
\mu_{h,v}
in §
4.3.1
. Here
\mu_{2}\in X_{*}(\mathbf{T}_{2})
is given by
t\mapsto\diag(t,1\dots,1)
. Set
\textbf{{Frob}}:=\mu(\varpi)
, see §
4.2
for the reason why we choose this notation. Let
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}\in\End_{\mathds{Z}[B]}\mathds{Z}[G/K]
be the
\mathbb{U}
-operator associated to
{\mathbf{1}_{I\mu(\varpi)I}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}(G\sslash I,\mathds{Z})}
.
By
[
Bou21b
, Theorem
]
, the operator
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}
is annihilated by the Hecke polynomial
H_{w}(X)=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}A_{k}X^{k}\in(\text{End}_{R[G]}R[G/K])[X]
.

Fix the classe
[1]=1\cdot K\in\mathds{Z}[G/K]
. By
[
Bou21c
, Lemma
]
, we have for every
k\geq 1

\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}([1])=\mathcal{U}_{\mu^{k}}([1])=\sum_{h\in I^{+}/\mu(\varpi^{k})I^{+}\mu(\varpi^{-k})}h\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1],\text{ where, $I^{+}={U}\cap I$}.

We fix for each element
[b]
in
\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi\mathcal{O}_{F}
a lift
b\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
(e.g. Teichmuller lift). Set
S_{k}:=\{a=\sum_{i}a_{i}\varpi^{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}\colon a_{i}=0\text{ for all }i\geq k\text{ and }[a_{i}]\in\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi\mathcal{O}_{F}\text{ for all }i<k\}
.

Lemma 4.5.1
.

For every integer
k\geq 1
, the collection

\left\{(u_{k,a},v_{k,b})=\left(\begin{pmatrix}1&a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
&1&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}1&b_{1}&\cdots&b_{n-1}\\
&1&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&1\end{pmatrix}\right)\right\}

for all
{(a,b)=\left((a_{1},\dots,a_{n}),(b_{1},\dots,b_{n-1})\right)\in S_{k}^{n}\times S_{k}^{n-1}}
forms a complete set of representatives for
I^{+}/\mu(\varpi^{k})I^{+}\mu(\varpi^{k})^{-1}
. Consequently, for every
k\geq 1

\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}([1])=\sum_{(a,b)\in S_{k}^{n}\times S_{k}^{n-1}}(u_{k,a},v_{k,b})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1].

Proof.

This is a consequence of the fact that
{\mu}(\varpi^{k})I^{+}{\mu}(\varpi^{-k})
equals

\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F}&\cdots&\cdots&\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F}\\
&1&\mathcal{O}_{F}&\cdots&\mathcal{O}_{F}\\
&&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&\ddots&\mathcal{O}_{F}\\
&&&&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\times\begin{pmatrix}1&\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F}&\cdots&\cdots&\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F}\\
&1&\mathcal{O}_{F}&\cdots&\mathcal{O}_{F}\\
&&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&\ddots&\mathcal{O}_{F}\\
&&&&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\subset I^{+}.

Any
g\in I_{1}^{+}
, can be written as

g=\begin{pmatrix}1&a_{1}&\cdots&a_{n}\\
&\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
