for some
h\in K_{1}
(which is upper triangular modulo
\varpi
) and
(a_{i})\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{n}
. Therefore

g{\mu}(\varpi^{k})I_{1}^{+}{\mu}(\varpi^{-k})=g\iota(h^{-1}){\mu}(\varpi^{k})I_{1}^{+}{\mu}(\varpi^{-k})=u_{a^{\prime},k}{\mu}(\varpi^{k})I_{1}^{+}{\mu}(\varpi^{-k})

with some
a^{\prime}=(a_{i}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{n}
. The right action of
{\mu}(\varpi^{k})I_{1}^{+}{\mu}(\varpi^{-k})
on
I_{1}^{+}
can only kill
\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F}
in each factor
a_{i}^{\prime}
. This shows that each class admits a representative of the form
u_{k,a^{\prime}}
that is unique modulo
\varpi^{k}
. Similar statement hold for
I_{2}^{+}
. In total, this shows that is
S_{k}^{n}\times S_{k}^{n-1}
is a complete set of representatives for the quotient
{\mu}(\varpi^{k})I^{+}{\mu}(\varpi^{-k})
.
∎

4.5.1.
Divisibility in
\mathds{Z}[H_{0}\backslash G/K]
and proof of Theorem
1.3.1

Consider now the following natural surjective homomorphisms of
\mathds{Z}
-modules over the group algebra
{\mathcal{H}(G\sslash K)[H]}
39
39

39

The
H\times\mathcal{H}(G\sslash K)
-equivariance is seen from the identification
\mathcal{H}(G\sslash K)\simeq\End_{\mathds{Z}[G]}\mathds{Z}[G\sslash K]
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{Z}[G/K]}
Z
[H^der\G/K]
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{Z}[H_{0}\backslash G/K]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{0}}

where,
H^{\der}:=\Hbf^{\der}(F)=\Delta(\SL_{n})(F)
and
H_{0}\subset H
is the normal subgroup
\det^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times})\supset H^{\der}
. We derive the local horizontal distribution relations (Corollary
4.5.4
) from the following result

Theorem 4.5.2

(Local congruence relations)
.

We have

{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot\phi_{0}([1])\equiv 0\mod q^{n-1}(q-1)R[H_{0}\backslash G/K],\quad\quad(n\geq 1).

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}\in\End_{\mathds{Z}[B]}\mathds{Z}[G/K]
,
{H}_{w}(\mathcal{U}_{\mu})=0
and
\textbf{{Frob}}\in T\subset B^{\prime}
. Set
\widetilde{H}_{w}(X):=H_{w}(q^{n-1}X)
. We have,

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot\phi_{0}([1])

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}\left((\widetilde{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})-{H}_{w}(\mathcal{U}_{\mu}))\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}\big(\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}A_{k}(q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{Frob}^{k}-\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k})\cdot[1]\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{n(n+1)}A_{k}\phi_{0}\left((q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{Frob}^{k}-\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k})\cdot[1]\right)

where, we have used the
\mathbf{G}
-equavariance of
\text{since }A_{k}\in\End_{R[G]}R[G/K]
for the last equality.
In order to prove Theorem
4.5.2
, it is sufficient to show the following lemma.∎

Lemma 4.5.3
.

For any integer
k\geq 1
we have

\phi_{0}\left((\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}-q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{Frob}^{k})\cdot[1]\right)\equiv 0\mod q^{k(n-1)}(q-1)\mathds{Z}[H_{0}\backslash G/K].

Proof.

In steps A,B and C of the proof, we will be working only mod
H^{\der}
and we will wait until step D to project our calculations mod
H_{0}
.

A.

We have,

\displaystyle\phi(\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}([1]))

\displaystyle=\sum_{{(a,b)\in S_{k}^{n}\times S_{k}^{n-1}}}\phi((u_{k,a},v_{k,b})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1])\quad\quad(\text{Lemma }\ref{cosrep2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(a,b)\in S_{k}^{n}\times S_{k}^{n-1}\end{subarray}}\phi((\iota(v_{k,b})^{-1}u_{k,a},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1])\quad\quad(\Delta(v_{k,b})\in H^{\der})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{(a,b)\in S_{k}^{n}\times S_{k}^{n-1}}}\phi((u_{k,a-b},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]),

where,
a-b:=(a_{1}-b_{1},\dots,a_{n-1}-b_{n-1},a_{n})\in S_{k}^{n}
and
{1_{n}=\diag(1,\dots,1)\in\GL_{n}(F)}
. By substituting
c=(a-b)\in S_{k}^{n}
40
40

40

We sum over
c=(a-b)
’s. For every fixed
c
there is
q^{k(n-1)}=|(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{n-1}|
choice of pairs
(a,b)\in(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{n}\times(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{n-1}
such that
c=a-b=(a_{1}-b_{1},\dots,a_{n-1}-b_{n-1},a_{n})
.
, we get

\displaystyle\phi(\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}([1]))=q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot\phi([1])+q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{{c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}}}\phi((u_{k,c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]),

where the first term in the right-hand-side is the contribution for
c=a-b=0
i.e. (
a_{i}=b_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
and
a_{n}=0
).
Therefore,

\maltese:=\phi((\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}-q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot[1])=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{{c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}}}\phi((u_{k,c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1])

B.

Define the following map

\varepsilon\colon S_{k}^{n}\to(\varpi^{\mathds{N}})^{n}

sending
c=(c_{1},\dots,c_{n})\in S_{k}^{n}
to
\underline{\varepsilon}_{c}:=(\varepsilon(c_{1}),\dots,\varepsilon(c_{n}))
such that

\varepsilon(c_{i}):=\varpi^{{\text{ord}}_{F}(c_{i})}
with
\varepsilon(0)=0
(note that
{\text{ord}}_{F}(c_{i})\leq{k-1}
and that
\varepsilon(c)=0
if and only if
\underline{\varepsilon}_{c}=0_{n}:=(0,\dots,0)
).

Put
\mathcal{E}:=\varepsilon(S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\})
. Note that we can view any
n
-tuple
\underline{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{E}
as a
n
-tuple
\underline{\varepsilon}\in S_{k}^{n}
. Set

\tilde{x}:=\begin{cases}x/\varpi^{{\text{ord}}_{F}(x)}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}\text{ if }x\in\mathcal{O}_{F}\setminus\{0\},\\
1\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

For every
c=(c_{i})\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}
consider the following two matrices

\overline{c}:=\diag\big(\tilde{c}_{n}^{n-1}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tilde{c}_{i}^{-1},\tilde{c}_{n}^{-1}\tilde{c}_{1},\dots,\tilde{c}_{n}^{-1}\tilde{c}_{i},\dots,\tilde{c}_{n}^{-1}\tilde{c}_{n-1}\big)\in K_{2}\cap\SL_{n}(F),

and

\underline{c}:=\diag\left(\tilde{c}_{n},\tilde{c}_{n}\tilde{c}_{1}^{-1},\dots,\tilde{c}_{n}\tilde{c}_{i}^{-1},\dots,\tilde{c}_{n}\tilde{c}_{n-1}^{-1},1\right)\in K_{1}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\phi\left((u_{k,c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle=\phi\left(\Delta(\overline{c})(u_{k,c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle(\text{since }\Delta(\overline{c})\in H^{\der})

\displaystyle=\phi\left((\iota(\overline{c})u_{k,c}\underline{c},\overline{c})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle(\text{since $\underline{c}\in K_{1}\cap T_{1}$})

\displaystyle=\phi\left((\iota(\overline{c})u_{k,c}\underline{c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle(\text{since $\overline{c}\in K_{2}\cap T_{2}$})

A direct calculation yields the equality

\iota(\overline{c})u_{k,c}\underline{c}=\diag\big(\tilde{c}_{n}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tilde{c}_{i}^{-1},1,\dots,1\big)u_{k,\underline{\varepsilon}_{c}}\in\GL_{n+1}(F).

For every
c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}
, set

\alpha(c):=(\tilde{c}_{n}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tilde{c}_{i}^{-1}\mod\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})\in(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\times}.

The above equality shows that for every
c=(c_{i}),c^{\prime}=(c_{i}^{\prime})\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}
:

\alpha(c)=\alpha(c^{\prime})\text{ and }{\varepsilon}(c)={\varepsilon}(c^{\prime})\Rightarrow\phi((u_{k,c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1])=\phi((u_{k,c^{\prime}},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]).

C.

Using this we continue the computation of
\maltese
by regrouping terms over
c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}
giving the same values by
\alpha
and
\varepsilon
.

\displaystyle\maltese

\displaystyle=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{{c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}}}\phi((u_{k,c},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1])

\displaystyle=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{\underline{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\beta\in S_{k}^{\times}}\sum_{\{c\in S_{k}^{n}\colon\alpha(c)=\beta,\,\varepsilon(c)=\underline{\varepsilon}\}}\phi\left((\diag\left(\beta,1,\dots,1\right)u_{k,\underline{\varepsilon}},1_{n})\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{\underline{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\beta\in S_{k}^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|\,\phi\left((\diag\left(\beta,1,\dots,1)u_{k,\underline{\varepsilon}},1_{n}\right)\,\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

where,
J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta):=\{c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}\colon\alpha(c)=\beta,\,\varepsilon(c)=\underline{\varepsilon}\}
.

D.

Now we project
\maltese\in\mathds{Z}[H^{\der}\backslash G/K]
into
\mathds{Z}[H_{0}\backslash G/K]
. This gives us

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(\maltese)

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}((\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}-q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot[1])

\displaystyle=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{\underline{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\beta\in S_{k}^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|\,\phi_{0}\left((\diag(\beta,1,\dots,1)u_{k,\underline{\varepsilon}},1_{n})\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{\underline{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\beta\in S_{k}^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|\,\phi_{0}\left((u_{k,\underline{\varepsilon}},\diag(\beta^{-1},1,\dots,1))\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle=q^{k(n-1)}\sum_{\underline{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{E}}\big(\sum_{\beta\in S_{k}^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|\big)\,\phi_{0}\left((u_{k,\underline{\varepsilon}},1_{n})\textbf{{Frob}}^{k}\cdot[1]\right)

We observe that the sum
\sum_{\beta\in S_{k}^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|
is equal to

|\{c\in S_{k}^{n}\setminus\{0_{n}\}\colon{\varepsilon}(c)=\underline{\varepsilon}|.

Let
c=(c_{i})
such that
{\varepsilon}(c)=\underline{\varepsilon}
, which means that for every
i
, we must have
{\text{ord}}_{F}(c_{i})={\text{ord}}_{F}(\varepsilon_{i})
. It implies that for each
i
such that
\varepsilon_{i}\neq 0
, the
\tilde{c}_{i}=c_{i}/\varpi^{{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{i}}
are such that
{\text{ord}}_{F}(\tilde{c}_{i})\leq k-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{i}
. Hence, by definition of the set
S_{k}
, the set defined by these elements is described as follows

\left\{\sum_{j\geq 0}^{j=k-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{j}}a_{j}\varpi^{j}\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}\colon a_{j}=0\text{ for all }j\geq k-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{j}\right\}.

This shows that there exist
q^{k-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{i}}-q^{k-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{i}-1}
possible choices for
c_{i}
for each such
i
(with
\varepsilon_{i}\neq 0
). Therefore,

\sum_{\beta\in(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|=\prod_{i}(q^{k-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{i}}-q^{k-1-{\text{ord}}_{F}\varepsilon_{i}}),

where the product is taken over the indices
1\leq i\leq n
such that
\varepsilon_{i}\neq 0
. Since this set is nonempty for any
\underline{\varepsilon}\neq 0_{n}
, we deduce that

\sum_{\beta\in(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\varpi^{k}\mathcal{O}_{F})^{\times}}|J(\underline{\varepsilon},\beta)|\equiv 0\mod(q-1),

and accordingly, for all
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\left((\mathcal{U}_{\mu}^{k}-q^{k(n-1)}\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot[1]\right)

\displaystyle\equiv 0\mod q^{k(n-1)}(q-1)R[H_{0}\backslash G/K].\qed

4.5.2.
Local horizontal relation

Recall that
H_{c}=\det^{-1}(1+\varpi^{c}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}})
, for
c\in\mathds{N}
.

Corollary 4.5.4
.

Set
x_{0}:=\phi([1])
. There exists
x\in R[H^{\der}\backslash G/K]^{H_{1}}
such that

{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot x_{0}=\Tr_{1,0}x\in R[H^{\der}\backslash G/K]^{H_{0}},

where,
\Tr_{1,0}\phi(v):=\sum_{h\in H_{0}/H_{1}}h\cdot\phi(v)
.

Proof.

The action of
H_{0}
on
\mathds{Z}[H^{\der}\backslash G/K]
factors through
H_{0}/H^{\der}
which we identify with
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}
through the determinant map.
We note that

{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot x_{0}\in R[H^{\der}\backslash G/K]^{H_{0}},

since, the induced action of
\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}
commutes with the induced action of
{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})
and fixes
\phi([1])
, as

\det(K\cap H)=\det K_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}
.

Write

{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot x_{0}=\sum_{y\in H^{\der}\backslash G/K}a_{y}y,

with only finitely many nonzero integral coefficients
a_{y}\in\mathds{Z}
.
The stabilizer of any
y\in H^{\der}\backslash G/K
in
H/H^{\der}\simeq F^{\times}
is of the form

1+\varpi^{c(y)}\mathcal{O}_{F_{v}}
for some integer
c(y)>0
that we call the conductor of
y
. We have,

\displaystyle{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot x_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in H^{\der}\backslash G/K}a_{y}y

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in H^{\der}\backslash G/K\\
c(y)=c\end{subarray}}a_{y}y

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in H^{\der}\backslash G/K\\
c(y)=0\end{subarray}}{a_{y}}y+\sum_{c\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H_{0}y\in H_{0}\backslash G/K\\
c(y)=c\end{subarray}}a_{y}\sum_{h\in H_{0}/H_{c}}h\cdot y

In the third equality, we may sum up over classes
H_{0}y
since
{H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot x_{0}
is
H_{0}
-invariant. In the last sum above, choose for each

H_{0}
-orbit
H_{0}y
with
c_{(}y)\geq 1
some
H_{1}
-orbit
H_{1}\tilde{y}\subset H_{0}y
. Set

x:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in H^{\der}\backslash G/K\\
c(y)=0\end{subarray}}\frac{a_{y}}{q-1}y+\sum_{c\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}H_{0}y\in H_{0}\backslash G/K\\
c(y)=c\end{subarray}}a_{y}\sum_{h\in H_{1}/H_{c}}h\cdot\tilde{y}.

By Theorem
4.5.2
,
(q-1)\mid a_{y}
if
c(y)=0
, which gives
x\in R[H^{\der}\backslash G/K]
with
\Tr_{1,0}x={H}_{w}(\textbf{{Frob}})\cdot x_{0}
.
∎

Remark 4.5.5
.

Denote the element
x
constructed above by
x_{v}
to keep track of its associated place
v\in\mathcal{P}_{sp}
.

4.6.
Global split distribution relations

4.6.1.
Definition of the norm-compatible system

For any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathcal{N}_{sp}^{r}
, set
\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\subset\mathcal{P}_{sc}
for the places of
F
defined by the prime ideals dividing
\mathfrak{f}
.
Define

\xi_{\mathfrak{f}}:=u(r)^{-1}\cdot\pi_{\text{cyc}}\big([g_{0,S}]\otimes[1]^{S}(\mathfrak{f})\big)\in\mathds{Q}[\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{G},K}(\mathbf{H})]

where,
[g_{0,S}]:=\mathbf{H}^{\der}(F_{S})g_{0,S}K_{S}
,
[1]^{S}(\mathfrak{f}):=\left(\otimes_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}x_{v}\right)\otimes\left(\otimes_{v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}[1]_{v}\right)
and

u(r)=\begin{cases}[E^{\times}\cap\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{\times}\mathfrak{O}_{1}^{\times}:F^{\times}]&\text{If }r=0,\\
1&\text{If }r\geq 1.\end{cases}

Proposition 4.6.1
.

For each
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathcal{N}_{sp}
, the field of definition
E_{{\mathfrak{f}}}
of
\xi_{\mathfrak{f}}
is contained in
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
the
\mathcal{K}
-transfer field of conductor
\mathfrak{f}
.

Proof.

Write
\xi_{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum a_{i}\mathfrak{z}_{g_{i}}
(
a_{i}\in\mathds{Q}
) with

g_{i,S}=g_{0,S},\,\forall v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\quad g_{i,v}=g_{0,v}

and
x_{v}=\sum_{i}a_{i}[g_{i}]_{v}
for all
v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}
. The stabilizer of
\mathfrak{z}_{g_{i}}
in
\mathbf{H}(\mathds{A}_{f})
contains (as in Lemma
3.7.1
)

\mathbf{H}(\mathds{Q})\cdot({K_{\mathbf{H},g_{0},S}^{Z}}\times K_{\mathbf{H}}^{S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}K_{i,\mathfrak{p}}),

for some open compact subgroups
K_{i,\mathfrak{p}}\subset K_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Therefore, the stabilizer of
\xi_{\mathfrak{f}}
in
\mathbf{T}^{1}(\mathds{A}_{f})
contains
41
41

41

Since by Corollary
4.5.4
, we have
[x_{v}]\in(\mathds{Z}[q_{v}^{\pm 1}][H_{v}^{\der}\backslash G_{v}/K_{v}])^{U_{v}^{1}(1)}
.

\mathbf{T}^{1}(\mathds{Q})\cdot(U_{g_{0},S}\times U_{\mathfrak{f}}^{S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}\times\prod_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}U_{v}^{1}(1))=\mathbf{T}^{1}(\mathds{Q})\cdot U_{\mathfrak{f}},

and accordingly,
E_{{g_{\mathfrak{f}}}}
is contained in
\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})
for which we have (see §
3.9
)

\displaystyle\text{Gal}(\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})/E)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{\mathbf{T}^{1}(\mathds{A}_{f})}{\mathbf{T}^{1}(\mathds{Q})U_{\mathfrak{f}}}\simeq\frac{\mathds{A}_{E,f}^{\times}}{E^{\times}\mathds{A}_{F,f}^{\times}\mathfrak{O}_{\mathfrak{f}}^{\times}}.\qed

Remark 4.6.2
.

Proposition
4.6.1
and Lemma
3.9.3
imply that
E_{\mathfrak{f}}
is also contained in
E(\mathfrak{c}_{1}\mathfrak{f})
. Therefore, by Corollary
3.9.4
any prime ideal of
E
above an ideal
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathcal{P}
that is prime to
\mathfrak{f}
is unramified in the extension
E_{\mathfrak{f}}/E
, i.e.

E_{\mathfrak{f}}\subset\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})\subset E(\infty)^{un,w_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

4.6.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.3.2

For any
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathcal{N}_{sp}^{r}
and any place
{v_{\circ}}\in\mathcal{P}_{sc}\setminus\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}
with prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\in\mathcal{P}_{sp}
, we show here that:

H_{{w_{\circ}}}(\Fr_{{w_{\circ}}})\cdot\xi_{\mathfrak{f}}=\Tr_{\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f})/\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})}\xi_{\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f}},

where,
H_{{w_{\circ}}}\in\mathcal{H}_{K_{\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}}}(\mathds{Z}[q_{v_{\circ}}^{\pm 1}])[X]
is the Hecke polynomial attached to
\Sh_{K}(\mathbf{G},\mathcal{X})
at the place
{w_{\circ}}
of the reflex field
E=E(\mathbf{G},\mathcal{X})
defined by
\iota_{v_{\circ}}
. Indeed,
H_{{w_{\circ}}}(\Fr_{w_{\circ}})\xi_{\mathfrak{f}}
is equal to:

\displaystyle=u(r)^{-1}\pi_{\text{cyc}}\big([g_{0,s}]\otimes(H_{{w_{\circ}}}(\Fr_{w_{\circ}})[1]_{v_{\circ}})\otimes(\otimes_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}x_{v})\otimes\left(\otimes_{v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup\{v_{\circ}\}}[1]_{v}\right)\big)

\displaystyle{=}u(r)^{-1}\pi_{\text{cyc}}\big([g_{0,s}]\otimes(\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},0}^{\times}}/{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},1}^{\times}}}\lambda\cdot x_{v_{\circ}})\otimes(\otimes_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}x_{v})\otimes\left(\otimes_{v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup\{v_{\circ}\}}[1]_{v}\right)\big)

\displaystyle=u(r)^{-1}\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},0}^{\times}}/{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},1}^{\times}}}\pi_{\text{cyc}}\big([g_{0,s}]\otimes(\lambda\cdot x_{v_{\circ}})\otimes(\otimes_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}}x_{v})\otimes\left(\otimes_{v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup\{v_{\circ}\}}[1]_{v}\right)\big)

\displaystyle=u(r)^{-1}\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},0}^{\times}}/{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},1}^{\times}}}\pi_{\text{cyc}}\big(\lambda\cdot([g_{0,s}]\otimes(\otimes_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup{v_{\circ}}}x_{v})\otimes\left(\otimes_{v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup\{v_{\circ}\}}[1]_{v}\right))\big)

\displaystyle{=}u(r)^{-1}\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},0}^{\times}}/{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},1}^{\times}}}\pi_{\text{cyc}}\big([g_{0,s}]\otimes(\otimes_{v\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup{v_{\circ}}}x_{v})\otimes\left(\otimes_{v\not\in S\cup\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{f}}\cup\{v_{\circ}\}}[1]_{v}\right)\big)^{\Art_{w_{\circ}}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=u(r)^{-1}\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},0}^{\times}}/{\mathcal{O}_{{v_{\circ}},1}^{\times}}}\xi_{\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f}}^{\Art_{w_{\circ}}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=u(r)^{-1}\Tr_{\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f})_{w_{\circ}}/\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})_{w_{\circ}}}\xi_{\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f}}

\displaystyle{=}\sum_{\sigma\in\text{Gal}(\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f})/\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f}))}\xi_{\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f}}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\Tr_{\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f})/\mathcal{K}(\mathfrak{f})}\xi_{\mathfrak{p}_{v_{\circ}}\mathfrak{f}},

where,
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Art_{w_{\circ}}\colon E_{w_{\circ}}}
A
×
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Gal}(E^{ab}/E).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Art_{E}}
∎
